L 
L Imports are an ES6/ES2015 standard for making the functionality of other modules available in your current module. In CommonJS this is implemented through the `require()` call which makes this ESLint rule roughly equivalent to its CommonJS counterpart `no-restricted-modules`.
T 159 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown

L 
L Why would you want to restrict imports?
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L * Some imports might not make sense in a particular environment. For example, Node.js' `fs` module would not make sense in an environment that didn't have a file system.
T 1 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown punctuation.definition.list.begin.markdown
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown
T 87 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 88 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 90 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 91 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 169 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L * Some modules provide similar or identical functionality, think `lodash` and `underscore`. Your project may have standardized on a module. You want to make sure that the other alternatives are not being used as this would unnecessarily bloat the project and provide a higher maintenance cost of two dependencies when one would suffice.

L ## Rule Details
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown

L 
L This rule allows you to specify imports that you don't want to use in your application.
T 87 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L It applies to static imports only, not dynamic ones.
T 52 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L ## Options
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown

L 
L This rule has both string and object options to specify the imported modules to restrict.
T 89 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L

L This option in `paths` is an array and can be used to specify the names of certain bindings exported from a module. Import names specified inside `paths` array affect the module specified in the `name` property of corresponding object, so it is required to specify the `name` property first when you are using `importNames` or `message` option.

L #### allowImportNames

L This option is an array. Inverse of `importNames`, `allowImportNames` allows the imports that are specified inside this array. So it restricts all imports from a module, except specified allowed ones.

L 
L In this example, `"!import1/private/*"` is not reincluding the modules inside `private` because the negation mark (`!`) does not reinclude the files if it's parent directory is excluded by a pattern. In this case, `import1/private` directory is already excluded by the `import1/*` pattern. (The excluded directory can be reincluded using `"!import1/private"`.)

L ## When Not To Use It
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L Don't use this rule or don't include a module in the list for this rule if you want to be able to import a module in your project without an ESLint error or warning.
